# Source Pack: By the Heel

**Series:** by-the-heel · compiled 2026-09-12 for the /devo-go build.
**Rule:** this pack is the ONLY citation pool for drafting (AUTHORING-SPEC §8). Every quote, story, lexical claim and video used in the seven days traces to a section below. Items marked DOWNGRADE, REJECTED or UNVERIFIED are recorded so they are NOT printed.

Contents:

1. Scripture, verbatim from `public/bibles/` (BSB, KJV, ASV, YLT, DARBY)
2. Hebrew / Greek lexicon and textual-context verification
3. All-sides dossier: Leviticus 11, the Christian traditions, Romans 14, and the agreements
4. Verified quotes (and rejected misattributions)
5. Verified primary-source stories (and rejected embellishments)
6. Verified videos (oEmbed + embed-check), and rejected mirrors

Copyright note: short quotations only from in-copyright works (Bonhoeffer, Douglas, Milgrom, Wenham, Schreiner, Kinzer, Rudolph, Nanos, Wright). Links to unlicensed scans used during verification have been removed from this pack; cite the printed editions.

---

# 1. Scripture (corpus pulls)

## Pull: BSB

## LEV 11:1-8 — BSB

1 The LORD spoke again to Moses and Aaron, telling them, 2 “Say to the Israelites, ‘Of all the beasts of the earth, these ones you may eat: 3 You may eat any animal that has a split hoof completely divided and that chews the cud. 4 But of those that only chew the cud or only have a divided hoof, you are not to eat the following: The camel, though it chews the cud, does not have a divided hoof; it is unclean for you. 5 The rock badger, though it chews the cud, does not have a divided hoof; it is unclean for you. 6 The rabbit, though it chews the cud, does not have a divided hoof; it is unclean for you. 7 And the pig, though it has a split hoof completely divided, does not chew the cud; it is unclean for you. 8 You must not eat their meat or touch their carcasses; they are unclean for you.

## LEV 11:41-47 — BSB

41 Every creature that moves along the ground is detestable; it must not be eaten. 42 Do not eat any creature that moves along the ground, whether it crawls on its belly or walks on four or more feet; for such creatures are detestable. 43 Do not defile yourselves by any crawling creature; do not become unclean or defiled by them. 44 For I am the LORD your God; consecrate yourselves, therefore, and be holy, because I am holy. You must not defile yourselves by any creature that crawls along the ground. 45 For I am the LORD, who brought you up out of the land of Egypt so that I would be your God; therefore be holy, because I am holy. 46 This is the law regarding animals, birds, all living creatures that move in the water, and all creatures that crawl along the ground. 47 You must distinguish between the unclean and the clean, between animals that may be eaten and those that may not.’”

## LEV 19:18 — BSB

18 Do not seek revenge or bear a grudge against any of your people, but love your neighbor as yourself. I am the LORD.

## LEV 20:24-26 — BSB

24 But I have told you that you will inherit their land, since I will give it to you as an inheritance—a land flowing with milk and honey. I am the LORD your God, who has set you apart from the peoples. 25 You are therefore to distinguish between clean and unclean animals and birds. Do not become contaminated by any animal or bird, or by anything that crawls on the ground; I have set these apart as unclean for you. 26 You are to be holy to Me because I, the LORD, am holy, and I have set you apart from the nations to be My own.

## GEN 25:21-34 — BSB

21 Later, Isaac prayed to the LORD on behalf of his wife, because she was barren. And the LORD heard his prayer, and his wife Rebekah conceived. 22 But the children inside her struggled with each other, and she said, “Why is this happening to me?” So Rebekah went to inquire of the LORD, 23 and He declared to her: “Two nations are in your womb, and two peoples from within you will be separated; one people will be stronger than the other, and the older will serve the younger.” 24 When her time came to give birth, there were indeed twins in her womb. 25 The first one came out red, covered with hair like a fur coat; so they named him Esau. 26 After this, his brother came out grasping Esau’s heel; so he was named Jacob. And Isaac was sixty years old when the twins were born. 27 When the boys grew up, Esau became a skillful hunter, a man of the field, while Jacob was a quiet man who stayed at home. 28 Because Isaac had a taste for wild game, he loved Esau; but Rebekah loved Jacob. 29 One day, while Jacob was cooking some stew, Esau came in from the field and was famished. 30 He said to Jacob, “Let me eat some of that red stew, for I am famished.” (That is why he was also called Edom.) 31 “First sell me your birthright,” Jacob replied. 32 “Look,” said Esau, “I am about to die, so what good is a birthright to me?” 33 “Swear to me first,” Jacob said. So Esau swore to Jacob and sold him the birthright. 34 Then Jacob gave some bread and lentil stew to Esau, who ate and drank and then got up and went away. Thus Esau despised his birthright.

## GEN 27:1-41 — BSB

1 When Isaac was old and his eyes were so weak that he could no longer see, he called his older son Esau and said to him, “My son.” “Here I am,” Esau replied. 2 “Look,” said Isaac, “I am now old, and I do not know the day of my death. 3 Take your weapons—your quiver and bow—and go out into the field to hunt some game for me. 4 Then prepare a tasty dish that I love and bring it to me to eat, so that I may bless you before I die.” 5 Now Rebekah was listening to what Isaac told his son Esau. So when Esau went into the field to hunt game and bring it back, 6 Rebekah said to her son Jacob, “Behold, I overheard your father saying to your brother Esau, 7 ‘Bring me some game and prepare me a tasty dish to eat, so that I may bless you in the presence of the LORD before I die.’ 8 Now, my son, listen to my voice and do exactly as I tell you. 9 Go out to the flock and bring me two choice young goats, so that I can make them into a tasty dish for your father—the kind he loves. 10 Then take it to your father to eat, so that he may bless you before he dies.” 11 Jacob answered his mother Rebekah, “Look, my brother Esau is a hairy man, but I am smooth-skinned. 12 What if my father touches me? Then I would be revealed to him as a deceiver, and I would bring upon myself a curse rather than a blessing.” 13 His mother replied, “Your curse be on me, my son. Just obey my voice and go get them for me.” 14 So Jacob went and got two goats and brought them to his mother, who made the tasty food his father loved. 15 And Rebekah took the finest clothes in the house that belonged to her older son Esau, and she put them on her younger son Jacob. 16 She also put the skins of the young goats on his hands and on the smooth part of his neck. 17 Then she handed her son Jacob the tasty food and bread she had made. 18 So Jacob went to his father and said, “My father.” “Here I am!” he answered. “Which one are you, my son?” 19 Jacob said to his father, “I am Esau, your firstborn. I have done as you told me. Please sit up and eat some of my game, so that you may bless me.” 20 But Isaac asked his son, “How did you ever find it so quickly, my son?” “Because the LORD your God brought it to me,” he replied. 21 Then Isaac said to Jacob, “Please come closer so I can touch you, my son. Are you really my son Esau, or not?” 22 So Jacob came close to his father Isaac, who touched him and said, “The voice is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau.” 23 Isaac did not recognize him, because his hands were hairy like those of his brother Esau; so he blessed him. 24 Again he asked, “Are you really my son Esau?” And he replied, “I am.” 25 “Serve me,” said Isaac, “and let me eat some of my son’s game, so that I may bless you.” Jacob brought it to him, and he ate; then he brought him wine, and he drank. 26 Then his father Isaac said to him, “Please come near and kiss me, my son.” 27 So he came near and kissed him. When Isaac smelled his clothing, he blessed him and said: “Ah, the smell of my son is like the smell of a field that the LORD has blessed. 28 May God give to you the dew of heaven and the richness of the earth— an abundance of grain and new wine. 29 May peoples serve you and nations bow down to you. May you be the master of your brothers, and may the sons of your mother bow down to you. May those who curse you be cursed, and those who bless you be blessed.” 30 As soon as Isaac had finished blessing him and Jacob had left his father’s presence, his brother Esau returned from the hunt. 31 He too made some tasty food, brought it to his father, and said to him, “My father, sit up and eat of your son’s game, so that you may bless me.” 32 But his father Isaac replied, “Who are you?” “I am Esau, your firstborn son,” he answered. 33 Isaac began to tremble violently and said, “Who was it, then, who hunted the game and brought it to me? Before you came in, I ate it all and blessed him—and indeed, he will be blessed!” 34 When Esau heard his father’s words, he let out a loud and bitter cry and said to his father, “Bless me too, O my father!” 35 But Isaac replied, “Your brother came deceitfully and took your blessing.” 36 So Esau declared, “Is he not rightly named Jacob? For he has cheated me twice. He took my birthright, and now he has taken my blessing.” Then he asked, “Haven’t you saved a blessing for me?” 37 But Isaac answered Esau: “Look, I have made him your master and given him all his relatives as servants; I have sustained him with grain and new wine. What is left that I can do for you, my son?” 38 Esau said to his father, “Do you have only one blessing, my father? Bless me too, O my father!” Then Esau wept aloud. 39 His father Isaac answered him: “Behold, your dwelling place shall be away from the richness of the land, away from the dew of heaven above. 40 You shall live by the sword and serve your brother. But when you rebel, you will tear his yoke from your neck.” 41 Esau held a grudge against Jacob because of the blessing his father had given him. And Esau said in his heart, “The days of mourning for my father are at hand; then I will kill my brother Jacob.”

## GEN 32:22-32 — BSB

22 During the night Jacob got up and took his two wives, his two maidservants, and his eleven sons, and crossed the ford of the Jabbok. 23 He took them and sent them across the stream, along with all his possessions. 24 So Jacob was left all alone, and there a man wrestled with him until daybreak. 25 When the man saw that he could not overpower Jacob, he struck the socket of Jacob’s hip and dislocated it as they wrestled. 26 Then the man said, “Let me go, for it is daybreak.” But Jacob replied, “I will not let you go unless you bless me.” 27 “What is your name?” the man asked. “Jacob,” he replied. 28 Then the man said, “Your name will no longer be Jacob, but Israel, because you have struggled with God and with men, and you have prevailed.” 29 And Jacob requested, “Please tell me your name.” But he replied, “Why do you ask my name?” Then he blessed Jacob there. 30 So Jacob named the place Peniel, saying, “Indeed, I have seen God face to face, and yet my life was spared.” 31 The sun rose above him as he passed by Penuel, and he was limping because of his hip. 32 Therefore to this day the Israelites do not eat the tendon which is at the socket of the hip, because the socket of Jacob’s hip was struck near that tendon.

## GEN 33:1-11 — BSB

1 Now Jacob looked up and saw Esau coming toward him with four hundred men. So he divided the children among Leah, Rachel, and the two maidservants. 2 He put the maidservants and their children in front, Leah and her children next, and Rachel and Joseph at the rear. 3 But Jacob himself went on ahead and bowed to the ground seven times as he approached his brother. 4 Esau, however, ran to him and embraced him, threw his arms around his neck, and kissed him. And they both wept. 5 When Esau looked up and saw the women and children, he asked, “Who are these with you?” Jacob answered, “These are the children God has graciously given your servant.” 6 Then the maidservants and their children approached and bowed down. 7 Leah and her children also approached and bowed down, and then Joseph and Rachel approached and bowed down. 8 “What do you mean by sending this whole company to meet me?” asked Esau. “To find favor in your sight, my lord,” Jacob answered. 9 “I already have plenty, my brother,” Esau replied. “Keep what belongs to you.” 10 But Jacob insisted, “No, please! If I have found favor in your sight, then receive this gift from my hand. For indeed, I have seen your face, and it is like seeing the face of God, since you have received me favorably. 11 Please accept my gift that was brought to you, because God has been gracious to me and I have all I need.” So Jacob pressed him until he accepted.

## ROM 14:1-23 — BSB

1 Accept him whose faith is weak, without passing judgment on his opinions. 2 For one person has faith to eat all things, while another, who is weak, eats only vegetables. 3 The one who eats everything must not belittle the one who does not, and the one who does not eat everything must not judge the one who does, for God has accepted him. 4 Who are you to judge someone else’s servant? To his own master he stands or falls. And he will stand, for the Lord is able to make him stand. 5 One person regards a certain day above the others, while someone else considers every day alike. Each one should be fully convinced in his own mind. 6 He who observes a special day does so to the Lord; he who eats does so to the Lord, for he gives thanks to God; and he who abstains does so to the Lord and gives thanks to God. 7 For none of us lives to himself alone, and none of us dies to himself alone. 8 If we live, we live to the Lord, and if we die, we die to the Lord. So whether we live or die, we belong to the Lord. 9 For this reason Christ died and returned to life, that He might be the Lord of both the dead and the living. 10 Why, then, do you judge your brother? Or why do you belittle your brother? For we will all stand before God’s judgment seat. 11 It is written: “As surely as I live, says the Lord, every knee will bow before Me; every tongue will confess to God.” 12 So then, each of us will give an account of himself to God. 13 Therefore let us stop judging one another. Instead, make up your mind not to put any stumbling block or obstacle in your brother’s way. 14 I am convinced and fully persuaded in the Lord Jesus that nothing is unclean in itself. But if anyone regards something as unclean, then for him it is unclean. 15 If your brother is distressed by what you eat, you are no longer acting in love. Do not by your eating destroy your brother, for whom Christ died. 16 Do not allow what you consider good, then, to be spoken of as evil. 17 For the kingdom of God is not a matter of eating and drinking, but of righteousness, peace, and joy in the Holy Spirit. 18 For whoever serves Christ in this way is pleasing to God and approved by men. 19 So then, let us pursue what leads to peace and to mutual edification. 20 Do not destroy the work of God for the sake of food. All food is clean, but it is wrong for a man to let his eating be a stumbling block. 21 It is better not to eat meat or drink wine or to do anything to cause your brother to stumble. 22 Keep your belief about such matters between yourself and God. Blessed is the one who does not condemn himself by what he approves. 23 But the one who has doubts is condemned if he eats, because his eating is not from faith; and everything that is not from faith is sin.

## ROM 15:1-7 — BSB

1 We who are strong ought to bear with the shortcomings of the weak and not to please ourselves. 2 Each of us should please his neighbor for his good, to build him up. 3 For even Christ did not please Himself, but as it is written: “The insults of those who insult You have fallen on Me.” 4 For everything that was written in the past was written for our instruction, so that through endurance and the encouragement of the Scriptures, we might have hope. 5 Now may the God who gives endurance and encouragement grant you harmony with one another in Christ Jesus, 6 so that with one mind and one voice you may glorify the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ. 7 Accept one another, then, just as Christ accepted you, in order to bring glory to God.

## MRK 7:14-23 — BSB

14 Once again Jesus called the crowd to Him and said, “All of you, listen to Me and understand: 15 Nothing that enters a man from the outside can defile him; but the things that come out of a man, these are what defile him.” [MISSING 16] 17 After Jesus had left the crowd and gone into the house, His disciples inquired about the parable. 18 “Are you still so dull?” He asked. “Do you not understand? Nothing that enters a man from the outside can defile him, 19 because it does not enter his heart, but it goes into the stomach and then is eliminated.” (Thus all foods are clean.) 20 He continued: “What comes out of a man, that is what defiles him. 21 For from within the hearts of men come evil thoughts, sexual immorality, theft, murder, adultery, 22 greed, wickedness, deceit, debauchery, envy, slander, arrogance, and foolishness. 23 All these evils come from within, and these are what defile a man.”

---

# 2. 01 — Lexicon and Historical-Context Verification

Series: Law and Freedom (Leviticus 11 / Romans 14 / Jacob and Esau / the cross)
Prepared: 2026-09-12. Research agent: Hebrew/Greek and historical context.

---

## 0. Method, status key, numbering

**Method.** Every lexical entry was fetched live from BibleHub (Strong's page, with BDB, NAS Exhaustive, Thayer where given) and every verse from the BibleHub interlinear (word, Strong's number, morphology). Greek Old Testament readings came from BibleHub's Swete LXX pages and, for Theodotion's Daniel, ellopos.net. Textual evidence came from BibleHub's parallel Greek editions, Metzger's _Textual Commentary_ (via bibliaplus.org), Meyer, the UBS apparatus as posted on earlywritings.com, and the published scholarship named below. Nothing in the "VERIFIED" lines rests on memory.

**Status key.**

- **VERIFIED**: the spelling, form, gloss, and references were confirmed on the named page.
- **DISPUTED**: the lexical facts are verified, but scholars read the point differently. Each position is named.
- **DOWNGRADE**: a popular claim goes beyond what the lexicon or the text supports. Do not print it as fact.
- **UNVERIFIED**: I could not reach the primary source in this pass. Do not print it as fact.

**Verse numbering.** BibleHub's interlinear uses English verse numbers. BDB (and printed Hebrew Bibles) use Hebrew numbers. Where they differ:

- Gen 32:20 / 24 / 25 / 28 / 30 / 31 / 32 (English) = Gen 32:21 / 25 / 26 / 29 / 31 / 32 / 33 (Hebrew).
- Hos 12:3-4 (Eng) = Hos 12:4-5 (Heb). Jer 9:4 (Eng) = Jer 9:3 (Heb). Ps 41:9 (Eng) = 41:10 (Heb). Ps 89:27 (Eng) = 89:28 (Heb) = LXX Ps 88:28.

---

## PART A — HEBREW

### A1. tamei — "unclean" **VERIFIED** (with one DOWNGRADE)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | טָמֵא (adjective, H2931); verb טָמֵא (H2930)                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Transliteration | ṭāmēʾ                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Pronunciation   | tah-MAY                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Strong's        | H2931 (adj., 88 occurrences); H2930 (verb, 161 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Gloss (BDB)     | adj. "unclean": 1 ethically/religiously; 2 ritually (persons, animals, things); 3 places. Verb: "be or become unclean"; Piel "defile, pronounce unclean"                                                                                    |
| Key occurrences | Lev 11:4 טָמֵא הוּא לָכֶם "it is unclean _to you_" (Adj ms); Lev 11:47 הַטָּמֵא "the unclean" (Art + Adj ms); Lev 11:44 וְלֹא תְטַמְּאוּ אֶת־נַפְשֹׁתֵיכֶם "do not make yourselves unclean" (Piel imperfect 2mp); Isa 6:5 "unclean of lips" |

Notes:

- The formula in Lev 11 is relational: "unclean **to you**" (לָכֶם, Lev 11:4). BDB lists the Lev 11 uses under "ritually."
- BDB labels the Lev 11 occurrences "(P)" and Lev 17-26 occurrences "(H)". That is the classic critical source assignment (see C5).

**DOWNGRADE — "unclean means dirty or unhealthy."** The lexicon gives a ritual and religious category, not a hygienic one. The hygiene theory is one explanation among several, and the text gives no health motive (see A1a).

#### A1a. Why were animals unclean? The range of explanations **DISPUTED**

- **Hygiene.** Named in surveys as a minority view; critics note the text gives no health motive (Christine Palmer, _ESV Expository Commentary_ vol. 1, excerpted by Crossway; Jonathan McIlvaine, RTS paper, citing Wenham).
- **Creation order / anomaly.** Mary Douglas, _Purity and Danger_ (1966): clean animals fit their created realm; anomalous creatures are unclean. McIlvaine notes Douglas revised her approach in _Leviticus as Literature_ (1999).
- **Ethical / reverence for life.** Letter of Aristeas (abstaining from blood tames violence); Jacob Milgrom (the laws restrain the taking of animal life). Reported via McIlvaine.
- **Allegorical.** Philo (chewing the cud = reflection; divided hoof = making distinctions); Clement of Alexandria. Reported via McIlvaine.
- **No stated reason / obedience.** Listed by Palmer as the "arbitrary" view.
- **Rabbinic note on Lev 11:45.** Lev 11:45 says "I am YHWH _who brings you up_ (הַמַּעֲלֶה, Hiphil participle, H5927) from Egypt." Bava Metzia 61b (school of R. Yishmael) reads "brings up" as elevation: had God brought Israel out of Egypt only so they would not defile themselves with creeping things, it would suffice. VERIFIED on Sefaria.
- **Lexical side note (VERIFIED, no source draws a sermon from it).** The same verb, עלה Hiphil participle (H5927), describes animals "bringing up" (chewing) the cud in Lev 11:4 (מַעֲלֵה גֵרָה). Do not present this as a known interpretation; I found no source that makes the link.

### A2. tahor — "clean" **VERIFIED**

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Spelling        | טָהוֹר (plene); טָהֹר (defective, Lev 11:47); verb טָהֵר (H2891)                                                                                             |
| Transliteration | ṭāhôr                                                                                                                                                        |
| Pronunciation   | tah-HOHR                                                                                                                                                     |
| Strong's        | H2889 (93 occurrences); verb H2891 (95)                                                                                                                      |
| Gloss (BDB)     | 1 ceremonially clean (animals, places, things, persons); 2 physically pure (pure gold, Exod 25:11); 3 ethically pure (Ps 51:12 Heb = 51:10 Eng, clean heart) |
| Key occurrences | Gen 7:2 clean animals in Noah's ark; Lev 11:47 הַטָּהֹר "the clean" (Art + Adj ms); Exod 25:11 pure gold; Ps 51:10 Eng                                       |

Notes:

- The clean/unclean animal distinction appears in Gen 7:2 and 8:20, long before Sinai (BDB lists both).
- Strong's says the verb means "properly, to be bright." BDB gives only cognates (Aramaic "brightness"). Treat "bright" as a Strong's etymology note, not as the meaning in Leviticus.

### A3. qadosh — "holy" (Lev 11:44-45) **VERIFIED** (etymology claim UNVERIFIED)

| Field                   | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling                | קָדוֹשׁ                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Transliteration         | qāḏôš                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Pronunciation           | kah-DOHSH                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Strong's                | H6918 (118 occurrences); verb קָדַשׁ H6942                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Gloss (BDB)             | "sacred, holy"; of God "as separate, apart, and so sacred, holy"; "separate from human infirmity, impurity, and sin" (cites Lev 11:44-45)                                                                                                                      |
| Morphology in Lev 11:44 | וְהִתְקַדִּשְׁתֶּם "and you shall consecrate yourselves" (Hitpael conjunctive perfect 2mp); וִהְיִיתֶם קְדֹשִׁים "and you shall be holy" (Adj mp, defective spelling); כִּי קָדוֹשׁ אָנִי "for holy am I" (Adj ms, plene spelling). Lev 11:45 repeats the pair |
| Other occurrences       | Lev 19:2; 20:26; Isa 6:3 (thrice); Exod 19:6 "holy nation"                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Notes:

- The order in Lev 11:44 is "holy ... for _holy_ am I": the adjective is fronted for emphasis.
- The spelling difference (קְדֹשִׁים defective for Israel, קָדוֹשׁ plene for God) is verified. Any midrash built on it was not checked. Do not cite one.

**UNVERIFIED — "the root literally means 'cut off' / 'set apart'."** BDB's usage gloss "separate, apart" supports "set apart" as a _meaning in use_. The pages I fetched give no etymology for the root. Say "holy: set apart for God," not "the root literally means cut."

### A4. sheqets — "detestable thing" (Lev 11) **VERIFIED** (two DOWNGRADES)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | שֶׁקֶץ; verb שִׁקֵּץ (Piel)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Transliteration | šeqeṣ                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Pronunciation   | SHEH-kets                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Strong's        | H8263 (11 occurrences); verb H8262 (7)                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Gloss (BDB)     | "detestation, detestable thing": of the ceremonially unclean; creatures forbidden as food: water animals without fins or scales (Lev 11:10-12), birds (11:13), winged swarmers (11:20, 23), creeping vermin (11:41-42). Verb: Piel "detest, make detestable"    |
| Key occurrences | Lev 11:10 שֶׁקֶץ הֵם לָכֶם "they are detestable to you" (N ms); Lev 11:11 וְשֶׁקֶץ יִהְיוּ לָכֶם ... וְאֶת־נִבְלָתָם תְּשַׁקֵּצוּ "they shall be detestable to you ... you shall detest their carcasses" (verb Piel imperfect 2mp); Lev 11:43 (verb); Isa 66:17 |

**DOWNGRADE 1 — "sheqets and tamei are synonyms."** In Lev 11 the two words mark different groups. _Tamei_ is used for land quadrupeds (11:4-8, 26-28) and for the eight swarmers whose carcasses defile by touch (11:29-31). _Sheqets_ covers water creatures, birds, and winged and ground swarmers. Lance Hawley, "The Agenda of Priestly Taxonomy: The Conceptualization of _Tame_ and _Sheqetz_ in Leviticus 11," _CBQ_ 77 (2015) 231-49, argues that _sheqets_ creatures are forbidden without contact impurity. Reported via McIlvaine; the article itself was not read.

**DOWNGRADE 2 — KJV "abomination" merges two Hebrew words.** KJV renders both שֶׁקֶץ (H8263) and תּוֹעֵבָה (H8441, to'ebah) as "abomination." They are different nouns. Deut 14:3 uses תּוֹעֵבָה of unclean food (BDB, VERIFIED). Lev 11 uses שֶׁקֶץ.

### A5. Ya'akov — "Jacob" **VERIFIED** (the name's meaning is DISPUTED)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | יַעֲקֹב                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Transliteration | Yaʿăqōḇ                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Pronunciation   | yah-ah-KOHV                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Strong's        | H3290 (349 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Key occurrences | Gen 25:26 וַיִּקְרָא שְׁמוֹ יַעֲקֹב "and he called his name Jacob" (after "his hand grasping Esau's heel"); Gen 27:36 (Esau's pun, see A7); Gen 32:28 (renaming); Jer 9:4 (pun, see A7) |

**DISPUTED / DOWNGRADE — "Jacob means deceiver."**

- Gen 25:26 links the name only to the **heel** (עָקֵב). The sense "supplanter/cheat" is **Esau's** hostile reading in Gen 27:36.
- Ellicott's Commentary (Gen 27:36): "Its right meaning is 'one who follows close upon another's heels'"; Esau "has had a lasting revenge; for the bad sense which he for the first time put upon the word Jacob has adhered to it."
- BDB: explained "from עָקֵב heel ... i.e. supplanter," but BDB also cites a Palestinian place-name in Egyptian records read as Y'qb-'r (= יַעֲקֹבאֵל) and a Babylonian personal name Ya'qub-ilu. BDB concludes the biblical name was "perhaps originally יעקבאל or the like."
- Wikipedia ("Jacob") reports Mari spellings _ya-ah-qu-ub-el_, about 1800 BC, and a theophoric sense "may El protect." Secondary source only.
- **Devotional wording:** "The text plays on Jacob's name as 'heel,' and Esau later twists it into 'cheat.'" Do not write "Jacob literally means deceiver."

### A6. aqev — "heel" (noun) **VERIFIED** (one DOWNGRADE)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | עָקֵב (absolute); עֲקֵב (construct)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Transliteration | ʿāqēḇ                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Pronunciation   | ah-KAYV                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Strong's        | H6119 (13 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Gloss (BDB)     | "heel, footprint, hinder-part"; "heel of man ... as object of attack from behind"                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Key occurrences | Gen 3:15 וְאַתָּה תְּשׁוּפֶנּוּ עָקֵב "and you shall bruise him [on the] heel" (N ms); Gen 25:26 וְיָדוֹ אֹחֶזֶת בַּעֲקֵב עֵשָׂו "his hand grasping at the heel of Esau" (Prep ב + N ms construct); Gen 49:17 (Dan, a serpent biting the horse's heels); Ps 41:9 Eng "has lifted up his heel against me" |

Notes:

- **Gen 3:15 and Gen 25:26 use the same noun.** BDB lists both under one entry, and BibleHub tags both H6119. The LXX also uses the same Greek noun in both: πτέρναν (Gen 3:15) and πτέρνης (Gen 25:26).
- John 13:18 (Judas at the supper) quotes Ps 41:9 with πτέρναν ("lifted his heel").

**DOWNGRADE — "Hosea 12:3 uses aqev (heel)."** Hosea 12:3 (Heb 12:4) uses the **verb** עָקַב (H6117, Qal perfect 3ms), not the noun. See A7.

### A7. aqav — "grasp the heel, supplant, overreach" (verb) **VERIFIED** (Jer 9:4 allusion DISPUTED)

| Pronunciation       | ah-KAHV                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Strong's            | H6117 (5 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Gloss (BDB)         | "follow at the heel; figuratively assail insidiously, circumvent, overreach"; a **denominative** verb from עָקֵב                                                                                                    |
| Gen 27:36           | הֲכִי קָרָא שְׁמוֹ יַעֲקֹב וַיַּעְקְבֵנִי זֶה פַעֲמַיִם "Is he not rightly named Jacob? He has _jacobed_ me these two times" (Qal waw-consecutive imperfect 3ms + 1cs suffix). LXX: ἐπτέρνικεν ("he has heeled me") |
| Hos 12:3 (Heb 12:4) | בַּבֶּטֶן עָקַב אֶת־אָחִיו "in the womb he took his brother by the heel" (Qal perfect 3ms)                                                                                                                          |
| Jer 9:4 (Heb 9:3)   | כִּי כָל־אָח עָקוֹב יַעְקֹב "for every brother surely supplants" (Qal infinitive absolute + Qal imperfect 3ms)                                                                                                      |

**DISPUTED — does Jer 9:4 allude to Jacob?**

- Yes: Ellicott ("Every brother Israelite is found to be a thorough-paced Jacob"); Barnes ("An allusion to the name of Jacob Genesis 27:36 ... 'every brother is a thorough Jacob'"); Cambridge Bible ("The Hebrew verb emphasizes the allusion to Genesis 27:36"); Matthew Poole; Gill.
- No: Pulpit Commentary ("There is nothing in the context to suggest an allusion to Genesis 27:36 (Jacob). The verb has its common sense of deceiving.").
- The spelling יַעְקֹב in Jer 9:4 matches the name's consonants. Most of the commentaries checked see a pun, but not all.

Cambridge Bible for Schools (Ryle): "'my blessing' is spelt in the Heb. with the same consonants as 'my birthright,' but with two letters transposed." The LXX renders the birthright as πρωτοτοκεῖα (compare B15).

### A9. berakah / barak — "blessing / bless" **VERIFIED** (one DOWNGRADE)

| Transliteration | bərāḵāh; bāraḵ                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Pronunciation   | beh-rah-KHAH; bah-RAKH                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Strong's        | H1293 (69 occurrences); H1288 (330 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Gloss (BDB)     | Noun: 1 blessing (of parent, of God); 2 source of blessing (Gen 12:2); 3 prosperity; 4 praise; **5 gift, present (Gen 33:11)**; 6 treaty of peace. Verb: Qal "kneel" (2 Chr 6:13; Ps 95:6) and passive participle "blessed"; Piel "bless"                                 |
| Key occurrences | Gen 27:27 וַיְבָרְכֵהוּ "and he blessed him" (Piel waw-consecutive imperfect 3ms + 3ms suffix); Gen 27:36 בִּרְכָתִי "my blessing"; **Gen 33:11** קַח־נָא אֶת־בִּרְכָתִי "please take my blessing" (N fs construct + 1cs), where Jacob presses his gift on Esau; Gen 12:2 |

**Verified connection a devotional can use.** In Gen 27:36 Esau complains that Jacob "has taken my blessing" (בִּרְכָתִי). In Gen 33:11 Jacob urges Esau, "take my blessing" (בִּרְכָתִי), meaning his gift (BDB sense 5). Same noun, same suffix.

**DOWNGRADE — "to bless literally means to kneel, so blessing is about kneeling."** BDB and Strong's list "kneel" and "bless" under one verb. The pages I fetched do not show how the meanings are related, and scholars dispute whether "kneel" and "bless" come from one root or two. TWOT (Oswalt) and TDOT (Scharbert) could not be fetched: UNVERIFIED. Do not build a point on "kneel."

### A11. "Hip/thigh" in Gen 32:25 — kaf yerekh **VERIFIED** (two DOWNGRADES)

| Field                     | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Phrase                    | כַּף־יֶרֶךְ "hollow/socket of the thigh"                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Words                     | כַּף kap̄ (H3709, 193 occurrences): "hollow or flat of the hand, palm, sole, pan"; NASB "socket" 4x. יָרֵךְ yārēḵ (H3409, 34 occurrences): "thigh, loin, side, base"                                                                      |
| Pronunciation             | kahf YEH-rekh                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Gen 32:25 Eng (Heb 32:26) | וַיִּגַּע בְּכַף־יְרֵכוֹ וַתֵּקַע כַּף־יֶרֶךְ יַעֲקֹב בְּהֵאָבְקוֹ עִמּוֹ "and he **touched** the socket of his thigh, and the socket of Jacob's thigh was dislocated as he wrestled with him"                                           |
| Morphology                | וַיִּגַּע Qal waw-consecutive imperfect 3ms of נָגַע (H5060) "touch"; וַתֵּקַע Qal waw-consecutive imperfect 3fs of יָקַע (H3363; BDB "be dislocated," Gen 32:26 Heb); בְּהֵאָבְקוֹ Niphal infinitive construct of אָבַק (H79) "wrestle" |
| BDB on the phrase         | כַּף הַיָּרֵךְ "hollow of the thigh" (Gen 32:26, 33 Heb), listed under sense 1a "outside of thigh"                                                                                                                                       |
| LXX                       | ἥψατο τοῦ πλάτους τοῦ μηροῦ ... καὶ ἐνάρκησεν "touched the flat of the thigh ... and it **grew numb**"                                                                                                                                   |

**DOWNGRADE 1 — "he struck Jacob's hip."** The Hebrew verb is נָגַע "touched" (BibleHub interlinear gloss). BSB's "struck" is interpretive. A light word produces a dislocation.

**DOWNGRADE 2 — "the angel touched the seat of Jacob's procreative power."** BDB does list "thigh = loins, as seat of procreative power" for _other_ passages (Gen 24:2; 46:26; Exod 1:5). BDB places Gen 32 under the ordinary "outside of thigh" sense. Do not present the sexual reading as the text's meaning.

**Wordplay note (VERIFIED as a commentator claim).** Ellicott: the rare verb אָבַק "occurs only here, and without doubt it was chosen because of its resemblance to the name Jabbok." Cambridge Bible: "very possibly intended to be a play on the name of the river Jabbok." Hosea 12 uses a different verb (שָׂרָה) for the struggle.

### A12. Yisrael — "Israel" (Gen 32:28) **VERIFIED** text / **DISPUTED** etymology

| Field                     | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling                  | יִשְׂרָאֵל                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Transliteration           | Yiśrāʾēl                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Pronunciation             | yis-rah-EL                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Strong's                  | H3478 (2,506 occurrences); verb שָׂרָה H8280 (2 occurrences: Gen 32:28 Eng; Hos 12:3 Eng)                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Gen 32:28 Eng (Heb 32:29) | לֹא יַעֲקֹב יֵאָמֵר עוֹד שִׁמְךָ כִּי אִם־יִשְׂרָאֵל כִּי־שָׂרִיתָ עִם־אֱלֹהִים וְעִם־אֲנָשִׁים וַתּוּכָל "Not Jacob shall your name be said any longer, but Israel, for you have striven (Qal perfect 2ms) with God and with men, and you have prevailed (Qal waw-consecutive imperfect 2ms)" |
| Hos 12:3-4 Eng            | וּבְאוֹנוֹ שָׂרָה אֶת־אֱלֹהִים "in his strength he strove with God"; וַיָּשַׂר אֶל־מַלְאָךְ וַיֻּכָל "he strove with the angel and prevailed; he wept and sought his favor"                                                                                                                    |
| LXX Gen 32:28             | ὅτι ἐνίσχυσας μετὰ θεοῦ "for you prevailed with God"                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

**How the text explains the name.** As "you have striven (שָׂרִיתָ) with God and with men and prevailed." That is the narrative's own explanation (paronomasia).

**DISPUTED — what the name means as a name.**

1. **"God strives / El persists" (God as subject).** BDB: "El persisteth, persevereth (or jussive Let El persist)," noting others' "contendeth" (Nestle) or "Let El contend" (Gray). NAS Exhaustive: "God strives." Cambridge Bible (Ryle): "on the analogy of similarly formed words, would be 'El persevereth'; ... exactly similar to Seraiah = 'Jah perseveres'; Ishmael = 'God hears.'" Names built this way normally make God the subject.
2. **"He strives with God" (the text's sense).** Cambridge Bible: "applied here to Jacob as 'the perseverer with God.'" Pulpit reports "wrestler with God" (Fürst, Keil, Kurtz, Lange).
3. **"Prince with God / he rules as God."** KJV "as a prince hast thou power with God"; Strong's "he will rule as God"; Matthew Henry, Ellicott, Poole, Gill "prince of God"; Pulpit reports "Prince of El" (Calvin, Ainsworth) and "God governs" (Kalisch). Cambridge Bible calls the derivation from _sar_ ("prince") "another suggested derivation."
4. **"El will rule."** John Day, reported by Wikipedia ("Israel (name)"); secondary only.
5. **Other proposals Gill rejects:** "the upright one of God," "the man that sees God."

**DOWNGRADE — "Israel means 'prince with God.'"** That comes from the KJV and older etymologies, not from the verb in Gen 32:28, which is שָׂרָה "strive/persist."

**Extra-biblical attestation.** Cambridge Bible: "Israel" appears "as is generally believed" on the Merneptah Stele (Ysir'r), about 1230 BC.

**Jewish reading.** Rashi on Gen 32:29 (Heb): the blessings would no longer be said to come "through supplanting and subtlety but through noble conduct and in an open manner" (Rosenbaum-Silbermann translation, Sefaria).

### A13. panim — "face" (Gen 32:30; 33:10) **VERIFIED**

| Field                     | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling                  | פָּנִים                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Transliteration           | pānîm                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Pronunciation             | pah-NEEM                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Strong's                  | H6440 (2,128 occurrences). Strong's: "Plural (but always as singular)"; also forms prepositions ("before")                                                                                                                                                        |
| Gen 32:20 Eng (Heb 32:21) | Four "face" uses in one verse: אֲכַפְּרָה פָנָיו בַּמִּנְחָה הַהֹלֶכֶת לְפָנָי וְאַחֲרֵי־כֵן אֶרְאֶה פָנָיו אוּלַי יִשָּׂא פָנָי "I will _cover_ his face with the gift that goes before my face; afterward I will see his face; perhaps he will lift up my face" |
| Gen 32:30 Eng             | פָּנִים אֶל־פָּנִים "face to face" (N mp)                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Gen 33:10                 | רָאִיתִי פָנֶיךָ כִּרְאֹת פְּנֵי אֱלֹהִים וַתִּרְצֵנִי "I have seen your face, like seeing the face of God, and you received me favorably"                                                                                                                        |

**Verified detail on Gen 32:20.** "Cover/appease" is אֲכַפְּרָה, Piel cohortative of כָּפַר (H3722), the root used for atonement in Leviticus. BDB glosses Gen 32:21 (Heb) "let me cover his face by the present (so that he does not see the offence, i.e. pacify him)." The "gift" is מִנְחָה (H4503): BDB sense 1 "gift, present" (Gen 32:14, 19, 21, 22; 33:10), elsewhere sense 3 "offering made to God."

**Caution.** It is fair to say Jacob uses the vocabulary of covering and gift-offering. Do not say "Jacob made atonement for his sin with Esau"; BDB's sense here is "pacify."

## PART B — GREEK

### B1. koinos — "common, ritually defiled" **VERIFIED** (one DISPUTED, one DOWNGRADE)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | κοινός; verb κοινόω (G2840)                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Transliteration | koinos                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Pronunciation   | koy-NOSS                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Strong's        | G2839 (14 occurrences); G2840 (14)                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Gloss (Thayer)  | 1 common, shared (Acts 2:44; 4:32; Titus 1:4; Jude 3); 2 "by a usage foreign to classical Greek ... opposed to ἅγιος ... hence unhallowed, _levitically unclean_" (Mark 7:2, 5; Rom 14:14; Heb 10:29; Rev 21:27; 1 Macc 1:47, 62) |
| Mark 7:2        | κοιναῖς χερσίν, τοῦτ᾽ ἔστιν ἀνίπτοις "with defiled hands, that is, unwashed" (Adj dative fem pl). Mark glosses the word for his readers                                                                                           |
| Acts 10:14      | οὐδέποτε ἔφαγον πᾶν κοινὸν καὶ ἀκάθαρτον "I have never eaten anything common and unclean" (Adj acc neut sg)                                                                                                                       |
| Acts 10:15      | ἃ ὁ θεὸς ἐκαθάρισεν, σὺ μὴ κοίνου "what God has cleansed, do not call common" (κοίνου = present active imperative 2s of κοινόω)                                                                                                   |
| Rom 14:14       | οἶδα καὶ πέπεισμαι ἐν κυρίῳ Ἰησοῦ ὅτι οὐδὲν κοινὸν δι᾽ ἑαυτοῦ ... ἐκείνῳ κοινόν "I know and am persuaded in the Lord Jesus that nothing is unclean in itself ... to that one it is unclean" (Adj nom neut sg, 3x)                 |

**DISPUTED — "koinos means clean food defiled by contact, while akathartos means an inherently unclean animal."**

- Held by Colin House, "Defilement by Association: Some Insights from the Usage of _koinos/koinoō_ in Acts 10 and 11," _AUSS_ 21 (1983) 143-53. This view is often used to argue that Rom 14:14 does not touch the animals of Lev 11.
- Against a strict split (all VERIFIED): 1 Macc 1:47 LXX θύειν ὕεια καὶ κτήνη κοινά ("to sacrifice swine and _common_ animals") uses κοινός beside pork. 1 Macc 1:62 has "not to eat κοινά." Thayer treats κοινός in Rom 14:14 as "levitically unclean." Rom 14:20 says πάντα μὲν καθαρά "all things are clean."
- **Devotional wording:** "koinos = ritually 'common,' unfit for holy use." Do not claim a hard technical split.

**DOWNGRADE — HELPS Word-studies (on BibleHub): koinos "is always used negatively ... except in Jude 1:3."** Thayer lists positive "shared" uses in Acts 2:44, Acts 4:32, and Titus 1:4 as well. HELPS overstates.

| Pronunciation   | as-theh-NEH-oh; as-theh-NAYSS                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Rom 14:1        | Τὸν δὲ ἀσθενοῦντα τῇ πίστει προσλαμβάνεσθε "welcome the one who is weak in faith" (present active participle acc masc sg + dative of respect)                                                               |
| Rom 14:2        | ὁ δὲ ἀσθενῶν λάχανα ἐσθίει "but the one who is weak eats vegetables" (present active participle nom masc sg)                                                                                                |
| 1 Cor 8:9       | τοῖς ἀσθενέσιν (Adj dative masc pl)                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Rom 15:1        | Ὀφείλομεν δὲ ἡμεῖς οἱ δυνατοὶ τὰ ἀσθενήματα τῶν ἀδυνάτων βαστάζειν "we who are strong ought to _bear_ the weaknesses of those without strength" (Paul counts himself among the strong; note βαστάζειν, B12) |
| Thayer          | Rom 14:1: "to be weak in faith ... to be doubtful about things lawful and unlawful to a Christian"; asthenēs in 1 Cor 8:7-9: "lacking in decision about things lawful and unlawful"                         |

**DOWNGRADE — "the weak are spiritually immature or less saved."** Thayer ties the weakness to uncertainty about what is permitted (food, days). Rom 14:23 connects it to acting "not from faith": ὁ δὲ διακρινόμενος ἐὰν φάγῃ κατακέκριται "the one who doubts is condemned if he eats" (VERIFIED). Paul commands welcome, not correction, in 14:1.

Identity of "the weak." Older commentators on BibleHub (Benson: "A believing Jew, not thoroughly informed of his Christian liberty"; Barnes; Poole) take them as Jewish believers. Modern alternatives were not verified in this pass. Name only these older sources if you name any.

**Gal 4:7 caution.** If a devotional quotes "heir of God _through Christ_," it is quoting the TR/KJV wording, not the critical text.

| Transliteration | diathēkē                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Pronunciation   | dee-ah-THAY-kay                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Strong's        | G1242 (33 occurrences)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Gloss (Thayer)  | 1 "a disposition, arrangement ... especially the last disposal which one makes of his earthly possessions after his death, a testament or will ... Heb 9:16f"; 2 "a compact, covenant ... very often in the Scriptures for בְּרִית (Vulg. _testamentum_)" |
| Heb 9:15        | διαθήκης καινῆς μεσίτης ... ἐπὶ τῇ πρώτῃ διαθήκῃ "mediator of a new covenant ... under the first covenant" (gen / dat fem sg)                                                                                                                             |
| Heb 9:16        | ὅπου γὰρ διαθήκη, θάνατον ἀνάγκη φέρεσθαι τοῦ διαθεμένου (διαθεμένου = aorist middle participle gen masc sg, "the one who made it")                                                                                                                       |
| Heb 9:17        | διαθήκη γὰρ ἐπὶ νεκροῖς βεβαία, ἐπεὶ μήποτε ἰσχύει ὅτε ζῇ ὁ διαθέμενος (literally "a diathēkē is firm _upon dead [ones]_, since it is never in force while the one who made it lives")                                                                    |

**The wordplay.** In ordinary Greek, διαθήκη was the normal word for a will. The LXX used it for Hebrew בְּרִית "covenant." Heb 9:15 moves from "covenant" to "inheritance" and "death." The "will" reading says the author exploits both senses in 9:16-17. The "covenant" reading denies any shift of sense. The debate is in C8.

### B10. katapetasma — "curtain, veil" **VERIFIED** (which curtain is DISPUTED)

| Field           | Data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Spelling        | καταπέτασμα                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Transliteration | katapetasma                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Pronunciation   | kah-tah-PEH-tahs-mah                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Strong's        | G2665 (6 occurrences: Matt 27:51; Mark 15:38; Luke 23:45; Heb 6:19; 9:3; 10:20)                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Etymology       | κατά + πετάννυμι "spread out" (NAS Exhaustive; Thayer)                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Thayer          | the Greek Bible, Philo, and Josephus name _both_ temple curtains this way: the outer one (Heb. מָסָךְ, Exod 26:37) and the inner one before the Holy of Holies (Heb. פָּרֹכֶת, Exod 26:31ff). "This latter [inner] καταπέτασμα is the only one mentioned in the N.T." |
| Mark 15:38      | Καὶ τὸ καταπέτασμα τοῦ ναοῦ ἐσχίσθη εἰς δύο ἀπ᾽ ἄνωθεν ἕως κάτω "and the curtain of the sanctuary was torn in two from top to bottom" (N nom neut sg)                                                                                                                 |
| Heb 9:3         | μετὰ δὲ τὸ δεύτερον καταπέτασμα σκηνὴ ἡ λεγομένη Ἅγια Ἁγίων "behind the _second_ curtain, the tent called the Holy of Holies"                                                                                                                                         |
| Heb 10:20       | διὰ τοῦ καταπετάσματος, τοῦτ᾽ ἔστιν τῆς σαρκὸς αὐτοῦ "through the curtain, that is, his flesh" (N gen neut sg)                                                                                                                                                        |

**DISPUTED — which curtain tore in Mark 15:38?** Daniel M. Gurtner, "The Rending of the Veil (Matt. 27:51a par): A Look Back and a Way Forward," _Themelios_ 29.3, surveys the views:

- **Inner curtain:** Grundmann, Keil, Bleek (lexical grounds); C. Schneider (cultic significance). Readers who link the Gospels to Hebrews (Calvin, Morris, Hagner) also take the inner curtain.
- **Outer curtain:** H. M. Jackson (visible to the centurion). David Ulansey, "The Heavenly Veil Torn: Mark's Cosmic _Inclusio_," _JBL_ 110 (1991) 123-25, argues from Josephus, _War_ 5.212-214, that the outer curtain showed a panorama of the heavens. I. H. Marshall favors the outer curtain for Luke.
- **Undecided:** C. E. B. Cranfield.
- Gurtner: the LXX calls _three_ tabernacle curtains καταπέτασμα, so lexical evidence alone cannot settle it.
- **Devotional wording:** Hebrews (6:19; 9:3; 10:20) clearly means the inner curtain. For Mark, say "the temple curtain." Do not assert which one as fact.

**Range of meanings (Gurtner's survey):** judgment on the temple (Davies-Allison, Evans, Brandon); access to God (Calvin, Kingsbury, Hagner); departure of God's Spirit (T. E. Schmidt); mourning (Daube, M'Neile); removal of barriers between peoples (J. B. Green); vindication of Jesus as Son (Origen).

| Gal 6:5         | ἕκαστος γὰρ τὸ ἴδιον φορτίον βαστάσει "each will carry his own load" (future). Different noun: βάρος (G922) in 6:2, φορτίον (G5413) in 6:5. No meaning distinction was verified |

- Mark 15:21 and Matt 27:32 use ἵνα **ἄρῃ** τὸν σταυρόν (αἴρω, aorist subjunctive).
- Luke 23:26 uses τὸν σταυρὸν **φέρειν** ὄπισθεν τοῦ Ἰησοῦ (φέρω, present infinitive, "behind Jesus").
- John 19:17 uses βαστάζων of **Jesus** and does not mention Simon.
- The idea of bearing is shared, but the Greek words are not. **Verified and usable:** Rom 15:1 and Gal 6:2 both use βαστάζω of bearing others.
- Luke 23:26 ὄπισθεν ("behind") resembles Luke 14:27 ὀπίσω ("after"), but the adverbs are different words. Do not claim a verbal match.

Comparative ("before / over all creation"): Thayer mentions this construal and refers readers to Lightfoot, "especially for the patristic interpretation."
- The Ps 89:27 rank sense is a verified biblical precedent for "firstborn" as supremacy.
- **Devotional wording:** "firstborn = the heir with priority and preeminence (Ps 89:27)." Do not say "firstborn _never_ means born first"; it does in Luke 2:7.

**Heb 12:23.** Thayer takes "the firstborn" as the Christian dead already in heaven. Other identifications exist but were not verified here. Present Heb 12:23 as "believers enrolled in heaven, sharing the firstborn's status," without pressing one view.

**Jacob/Esau tie (VERIFIED via Thayer's citation of Exod 4:22):** Israel is called God's "firstborn son."
